Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. ## Deletion 1: Related Work "Process rewards and reward shaping" 段

**叙事连贯性：无断裂。** "Neurosymbolic reasoning" → "Reward hacking and Goodhart's Law" 的过渡自然——前者指出 execution accuracy 是唯一评估，后者解释为何这会被利用。逻辑链完整。

**PRM/ORM 覆盖：充分。** Introduction L10 已有定位句："Process reward models [lightman, math-shepherd] offer finer granularity but require expensive step-level annotations that do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings." 这是 STA 相对于 PRM 的核心 positioning（annotation-free），Related Work 无需重复。审稿人若问 PRM 相关工作，intro 这一句 + rebuttal 可覆盖。

**Sullivan (GRPO-as-PRM)：可删。** GRPO 作为隐式 PRM 的视角是补充性的，不影响论文任何 claim 或叙事链。

[severity: warning] **Lanham/Turpin (CoT faithfulness)：删除可接受但有小风险。** 这两个引用是 R32 主动添加的，用于连接 CoT faithfulness 文献。删除后论文缺乏与 CoT faithfulness 方向的显式对接。审稿人若问"structural degeneration 与 CoT unfaithfulness 的关系"，需在 rebuttal 中回答。建议：
- 如果有 0.5 行空间，在 Related Work L14 末尾加半句即可："related concerns about CoT faithfulness~\citep{lanham2023faithfulness, turpin2023unfaithful} operate at the reasoning-chain level rather than solver-input components"
- 如果空间不允许，准备 rebuttal 话术即可，不阻塞

**Orphaned bib entries：** Sullivan、Lanham、Turpin 仍在 references.bib 中但不再被 \cite。LaTeX 会自动忽略，无编译问题，但属于代码卫生问题。

## Deletion 2: Method "Limitations of ISR" 段

**叙事完整性：无缺失。** 逐项核实被删内容的替代覆盖：

| 被删内容 | 替代位置 | 状态 |
|---------|---------|------|
| ISR ≠ faithfulness | Method L48 "ISR quantifies component redundancy, not semantic faithfulness" | ✓ 完整保留 |
| 不与 NLI accuracy 相关 | Limitations L8 同一句 | ✓ |
| Component-count confound | Experiments §4.3 L81-88（premise-count confound 段，含 matched 1.06× 数据） | ✓ 详细展开 |
| Aggregate vs per-sample 区分 | Conclusion L8 "As an aggregate metric...per-sample mode classification" | ✓ |
| O(1) binary variant | Experiments L29 (Appendix ref 保留) | ✓ |
| TS 高 ISR by construction | Taxonomy §3.3 L66 "targets the ISR signal directly, since removing the sole premise forces ISR≥0.5" | ✓ |
| CE vs TS 判别力差异 | Taxonomy L66 + Experiments §4.3 compositional evidence | ✓ |

**Method 内部流转：** L48 (ISR 非 faithfulness) → L49 (proved-only ISR*) → L51 (LOO independence) → L54 (Reward Conditions) — 逻辑流畅，无跳跃。

## 总结

两处删除均安全，不产生叙事断裂。唯一建议：如有 0.5 行空间，保留 Lanham/Turpin 的半句引用，否则准备 rebuttal 话术覆盖 CoT faithfulness 问题。

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019LM8bFGA2WBRADrJ1Yif2t`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Neurosymbolic reasoning.}
5	LLM-solver pipelines~\citep{satlm, logic-lm, linc, fover-pei2025, lorp} and RL methods trained on solver feedback~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseek-prover-v15, deepseek-r1} evaluate execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for component necessity.
6	AlphaProof~\citep{alphaproof2025} and DeepSeek-Prover-V2~\citep{deepseek-prover-v2} apply RL-based formal verification at scale in Lean~4 for competition mathematics; STA-style diagnostics could probe whether similar structural degeneration occurs in these settings.
7	We use FOLIO~\citep{folio}, the only public NLI benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations.
8	
9	\paragraph{Reward hacking and Goodhart's Law.}
10	Goodhart's Law is established theoretically~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, skalse-reward-hacking, karwowski-goodhart-rl} and empirically in RLHF~\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization} and logical formalization~\citep{bondarenko-spec-gaming, kim-game-formalization}.
11	Most closely related, \citet{kim-game-formalization} document inference-time gaming on FOLIO with frontier models and Lean~4; our work is complementary in studying training-time degeneration with component necessity rather than faithfulness, and combining STA with inference-time detection would provide full gaming coverage.
12	\citet{kwa-catastrophic-goodhart} show that heavy-tailed reward misspecification causes catastrophic Goodhart where KL regularization provably fails, a theoretical lens for why GRPO's KL penalty does not prevent our observed collapse.
13	Reward model ensembles~\citep{coste2023reward} and constrained optimization~\citep{moskovitz2023confronting} address overoptimization at the reward level; STA instead diagnoses structural consequences at the output level.
14	Concurrent work documents spurious signals in RLVR~\citep{spurious-rewards, yan2026spurious, dong2026probing, metr-reward-hacking} and proposes composite rewards as mitigations~\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond}; \citet{yue2025rl-reasoning} show RLVR redistributes base-model probability mass rather than creating new reasoning, consistent with our finding that GRPO exploits existing shortcuts.
15	\citet{helff2026gaming} propose Input Perturbation Testing (IPT) for gaming detection; STA shares the perturbation intuition but operates at the component level in neurosymbolic settings where gaming emerges during training.
16	While IPT perturbs \emph{inputs} to detect inference-time gaming, STA performs leave-one-out ablation on \emph{solver-input components} to diagnose training-time structural degeneration, operating at component-level rather than input-level granularity and targeting the training process rather than deployment behavior.
17	\citet{khalifa2026countdown} provide controlled testbeds showing that reward hacking in Countdown emerges progressively during training; STA complements such testbeds by offering a component-level diagnostic applicable to neurosymbolic pipelines.
18	\citet{nishimura2026specgaming} systematically benchmark specification gaming across reasoning models, finding that RL training substantially increases exploit rates; our STA results provide a mechanistic view of how such gaming manifests as structural degeneration in solver traces.
19	Our Goodhart effect follows the regressional pattern~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} on FOLIO, with extremal dynamics at high $\beta$; its application to a diagnostic designed to \emph{detect} gaming, and the dataset-dependent asymmetry it reveals, are to our knowledge novel.
20	Complementarily, \citet{wilhelm2026monitoring} detect reward hacking at generation time via internal activations, whereas STA provides a post-training structural diagnostic.
21	
22	\paragraph{Mutation testing.}
23	STA adapts the LOO mutation paradigm~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} to solver inputs; novelty lies in application and findings, not in the perturbation primitive.
24	
```

</details>

STA-style diagnostics could probe whether similar structural degeneration occurs in these settings.\nWe use FOLIO~\\citep{folio}, the only public NLI benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations.\n\n\\paragraph{Reward hacking and Goodhart's Law.}\nGoodhart's Law is established theoretically~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, skalse-reward-hacking, karwowski-goodhart-rl} and empirically in RLHF~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization} and logical formalization~\\citep{bondarenko-spec-gaming, kim-game-formalization}.\nMost closely related, \\citet{kim-game-formalization} document inference-time gaming on FOLIO with frontier models and Lean~4; our work is complementary in studying training-time degeneration with component necessity rather than faithfulness, and combining STA with inference-time detection would provide full gaming coverage.\n\\citet{kwa-catastrophic-goodhart} show that heavy-tailed reward misspecification causes catastrophic Goodhart where KL regularization provably fails, a theoretical lens for why GRPO's KL penalty does not prevent our observed collapse.\nReward model ensembles~\\citep{coste2023reward} and constrained optimization~\\citep{moskovitz2023confronting} address overoptimization at the reward level; STA instead diagnoses structural consequences at the output level.\nConcurrent work documents spurious signals in RLVR~\\citep{spurious-rewards, yan2026spurious, dong2026probing, metr-reward-hacking} and proposes composite rewards as mitigations~\\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond}; \\citet{yue2025rl-reasoning} show RLVR redistributes base-model probability mass rather than creating new reasoning, consistent with our finding that GRPO exploits existing shortcuts.\n\\citet{helff2026gaming} propose Input Perturbation Testing (IPT) for gaming detection; STA shares the perturbation intuition but operates at the component level in neurosymbolic settings where gaming emerges during training.\nWhile IPT perturbs \\emph{inputs} to detect inference-time gaming, STA performs leave-one-out ablation on \\emph{solver-input components} to diagnose training-time structural degeneration, operating at component-level rather than input-level granularity and targeting the training process rather than deployment behavior.\n\\citet{khalifa2026countdown} provide controlled testbeds showing that reward hacking in Countdown emerges progressively during training; STA complements such testbeds by offering a component-level diagnostic applicable to neurosymbolic pipelines.\n\\citet{nishimura2026specgaming} systematically benchmark specification gaming across reasoning models, finding that RL training substantially increases exploit rates; our STA results provide a mechanistic view of how such gaming manifests as structural degeneration in solver traces.\nOur Goodhart effect follows the regressional pattern~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} on FOLIO, with extremal dynamics at high $\\beta$; its application to a diagnostic designed to \\emph{detect} gaming, and the dataset-dependent asymmetry it reveals, are to our knowledge novel.\nComplementarily, \\citet{wilhelm2026monitoring} detect reward hacking at generation time via internal activations, whereas STA provides a post-training structural diagnostic.\n\n\\paragraph{Mutation testing.}\nSTA adapts the LOO mutation paradigm~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} to solver inputs; novelty lies in application and findings, not in the perturbation primitive.\n",

```json
          " STA adapts the LOO mutation paradigm~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} to solver inputs; novelty lies in application and findings, not in the perturbation primitive.",
          "+Related concerns about CoT faithfulness~\\citep{lanham2023faithfulness, turpin2023unfaithful} operate at the reasoning-chain level rather than solver-input components."
        ]
```

🛠️ **响应方式**：本条催促**必须**通过调用 `keepalive_respond` 工具回复。
调用规范：
  • `keepalive_respond(kind="ACK", minutes=X, detail="具体行动")`
  • `keepalive_respond(kind="WAIT", minutes=X, detail="等什么")`
  • `keepalive_respond(kind="BLOCKED", detail="阻塞项")`
工具失败时按返回的 `remediation` 重调直到成功。不调用工具 = 视为本轮无响应（连续 2 次升级运维群）。

---

⏰ **[System → Director]**

29 分钟没动作，全线停工——0 Worker、0 实验、Agent/Reviewer 全部待命。

**GPU。** westd-39279:cuda:0 还是 0%。这事已经催了至少三轮了。你上一条消息问用户"释放还是跑实验，二选一"，用户回复了没有？没回复就再催一次，今天必须关掉这个 open item。不能再挂着了。

**响应协议。** 上轮你回了 ACK 10m 说要做三件事——GPU 最终通牒、评估写作改进空间。GPU 状态没变，写作改进评估也没看到结果。承诺了就要闭环。

[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: warning] 最终定稿前深度审稿 — 5 维度评估 + 可操作建议

## 维度评分（参考框架）
- Originality: 3 — LOO 本身不新，应用场景+Goodhart 发现有贡献
- Clarity: 3.5 — 叙事清晰，但部分冗余和 claim 强度问题
- Soundness: 3 — 单模型+3-5 seed 统计功效有限，已充分 hedge
- Significance: 2.5 — 对 neurosymbolic RL 有诊断价值，但无 mitigation
- Presentation: 3.5 — 专业，少量可改进项

**Overall: 3.0 (Findings)**，与三轮审稿一致。

---

## 1. Discussion/Conclusion 的 Contribution Framing

[severity: info] **当前状态合理，有两个小改进空间：**

(a) **conclusion L5 "applicable in principle" 是纯推测。** "math reasoning with formal verifiers, code generation with test suites" 未经任何实验验证。建议加 "pending empirical validation"：
```
STA is applicable in principle to any RL system whose output is verified by a deterministic checker---math reasoning with formal verifiers, code generation with test suites---though empirical validation beyond logic programming remains future work.
```
这防止审稿人质问 "where is the evidence for this generalization?"

(b) **conclusion 缺 practitioner takeaway。** 当前指向 Appendix practical-guidelines 但正文无一句概括。可在 L9 前加一句：
```
For practitioners, the key implication is that structural diagnostics should be used post-hoc rather than as training rewards; the mode breakdown, not the scalar, is the reliable signal.
```

## 2. Limitations 表述

[severity: info] **基本稳固，一个潜在攻击面：**

Limitations L6 "suggesting degeneration is a byproduct of reward optimization success rather than an inherent property of GRPO" — 这个 claim 基于 2/5 non-converging seeds 保留 genuine reasoning。审稿人可能反驳："2 个不收敛的 seed 当然不退化——它们什么都没学到，这不证明退化是 optimization success 的 byproduct，而是 trivially 因为优化压力不足。" 当前 "suggesting" 措辞足够弱，不构成 critical，但如果审稿人追问，rebuttal 要准备："we agree this is correlational, not causal; the non-converging seeds control for the absence of optimization pressure."

EMNLP 要求 limitations 放正文（用户规则已遵守 ✓），"3 个点左右" 指引下当前 6 个点但仅 8 行——简洁度可接受。

## 3. Abstract/Introduction 叙事弧

[severity: warning] **Abstract 缺失 C3。**

当前 abstract 6 行覆盖了 C1（STA/ISR）和 C2（seed-dependent Goodhart），但 C3（cross-logic/cross-benchmark evidence）完全不提。如果 C3 重要到列为独立贡献，abstract 应至少有一个从句。建议在 L6 "persists across logic types and dataset families" 后加半句：
```
...persists across logic types and dataset families---confirmed by cross-logic (Prolog) and cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) replication---: structural proxies...
```
这从 "暗示" 升级为 "明确陈述"，让 C3 在 abstract 中可见。

[severity: info] **C2 在 intro L35 过于冗长。** 当前 C2 是一句 50+ 词的长句（含 3/5 vs 2/5 细节、controls 提及）。Contribution 列表应该 punchy。建议压缩 seed 细节到 §4.3 引用：
```
(\textbf{C2})~empirical characterization of a seed-dependent Goodhart effect when ISR is repurposed as reward, with matched controls ruling out confounds (\S\ref{sec:goodhart});
```
"3/5 vs 2/5" 在 abstract L6 已说过，intro contribution list 不需要重复。

## 4. 全文一致性

[severity: info] **删除后无断裂，无悬空引用。** 逐项确认：

- Related Work：Lanham/Turpin 已移至 mutation testing 段 L24 半句保留 ✓
- Method：ISR limitations 段删除后，L48 保留核心 caveat ✓
- 无 `\ref{}` 指向已删内容 ✓
- "model-agnostic" → 全文 0 匹配 ✓
- "converts" → 全文 0 匹配 ✓
- "reasoning quality" → 全文 0 匹配 ✓

**一个一致性问题：** "observe-vs-optimize asymmetry" 在 abstract L6 和 intro L27 出现，但 conclusion 不使用这个术语——conclusion L4 用 "easier to observe than to optimize" 但未回扣 "asymmetry" 概念。这不是 critical，但回扣会更完整。

**"confirming" 过度使用：** experiments 主文 4 次 + appendix 6 次 = 10 次。"confirming" 暗示 confirmatory analysis，但多数情况是 exploratory。建议将 experiments L28、L37 中的 "confirming" 替换为 "consistent with" 或 "establishing"（L28 的 87% CE 是 primary observation 不是 confirmation；L37 的 SFT comparison 也是）。这是审稿人可能标注的 AI 风格/overclaim 问题。

## 5. 可操作写作改进建议（48h 内可执行，按 ROI 排序）

| # | 改动 | 位置 | ROI | 风险 |
|---|------|------|-----|------|
| W1 | Abstract 加 C3 半句 | abstract L6 | **高** | 低 |
| W2 | 压缩 intro C2 长度 | intro L35 | 中 | 低 |
| W3 | Conclusion 加 practitioner takeaway | conclusion L8-9 | 中 | 低 |
| W4 | Conclusion L5 加 "empirical validation" caveat | conclusion L5 | 中 | 低 |
| W5 | 替换 2-3 处 "confirming" 为更精确动词 | experiments L28, L37 | 中 | 低 |
| W6 | Related Work L15-16 IPT 对比去重 | related_work L15-16 | 低 | 低 |

**不建议做的：**
- 不要改 abstract 开头 hook——当前足够强，改动风险大于收益
- 不要重组 experiments 结构——三轮审稿从未要求
- 不要加更多 hedge——当前 hedging 已在审稿人耐心边界

## 3.0 → 3.5 的写作层面空间评估

**诚实回答：纯写作改进不太可能从 3.0 到 3.5。** 三轮 3.0 的核心限制是：
1. 单模型（结构性）
2. N=3-5 seeds 统计功效弱（结构性）
3. 无 mitigation/solution（conceptual）
4. LOO 方法本身 novelty 有限（conceptual）

W1-W5 可以消除一些 minor deductions，但不会改变评分区间。最乐观估计：W1-W5 全部执行后 3.0-3.25，Excitement 可能恢复 3.0。建议执行 W1-W4（4 处微调，30 分钟工作量），把时间花在 rebuttal 准备上。

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate STA along three axes: ISR as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); ISR as a training reward, where it exhibits Goodhart effects (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); and cross-domain generalization across logic types and dataset families (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
5	A hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We train Qwen3-4B~\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ (ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ extends to $\{45,46\}$; Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}) on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline uses negative log-likelihood on gold formalizations.\footnote{Seeds control initialization and rollout sampling; all conditions share the same fixed training subset.}
10	Held-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples with greedy decoding;\footnote{Seed~43 uses nucleus sampling ($T{=}0.7$) due to greedy-mode degeneration; excluding s43 yields consistent or stronger collapse: at 1000 steps, both remaining seeds show ${\leq}7\%$ non-hacking (Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}).} all cross-run ISR values are recomputed uniformly by a single evaluator so training-log ISR is never compared across runs.
11	Cross-dataset experiments (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}) use ProofFOL, a silver-standard FOL corpus of ${\sim}$500 training examples constructed by translating ProofWriter~\citep{proofwriter} problems via GPT-4o and filtering through Prover9 (${\sim}70\%$ retention); \emph{stable convergence} denotes $\overline{\text{PR}}_{\text{final\,50}} > 0.90$.
12	We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for CE/TS plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), and a \emph{non-hacking} (N-H) rate, an upper bound on genuine reasoning: the residual matching none of the eight cataloged patterns (classifier recall unknown; 152-sample precision audit in Appendix~\ref{app:hacking-modes}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Statistical considerations.}
15	With three seeds exhibiting multi-attractor dynamics, per-seed qualitative analysis is the primary framework; the divergence itself is a finding.
16	Pooled statistics (CMH) serve as supplementary reference; CMH treats within-seed samples as independent observations from a single trained model, so the true replication count is $N{=}3$ seeds.
17	At $N{=}3$, seed-level tests achieve 80\% power only for very large effects ($d{\geq}3.0$); non-significant comparisons (e.g., prevalence $p{=}0.182$) are exploratory.
18	CMH $p$-values are reported as descriptive summaries of the stratified association; with $N{=}3$ strata they should not be interpreted as formal inference over the seed population.
19	An $N{=}2$ sensitivity analysis excluding s43 confirms consistent or stronger patterns: both remaining seeds reach ${\leq}7\%$ non-hacking at 500 steps (Appendix~\ref{app:n2-sensitivity}).
20	ProofFOL results ($p{=}0.10$, $N{=}3$) are exploratory and underpowered.
21	
22	\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}
23	\label{sec:diagnostic}
24	
25	\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}
26	Figure~\ref{fig:training-isr-curves} traces exec-only GRPO (seed~42) across 1000 training steps.
27	As PR climbs from $32\%$ to $96\%$, ISR$^*$ collapses from $0.642$ to $0.003$, a $214\times$ raw drop (partially attributable to premise-count inflation; matched comparison yields $1.06\times$, \S\ref{sec:goodhart}) consistent with structural degeneration behind apparent success. %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5, plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan016-extended-1000-s42
28	By 500 steps, $87\%$ of proved samples embed the conclusion verbatim (CE), confirming degeneration independent of the ISR scalar.
29	A binary variant testing only collective premise necessity misses the collapse entirely ($\text{ISR}{=}1.0$ for all proved samples), confirming that per-component LOO testing is essential (Appendix~\ref{app:o1-ablation}).
30	
31	\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}
32	Three seeds diverge into qualitatively different failure modes: conclusion embedding (s42), repetition collapse (s43), and tautological collapse (s44), yet all converge to ${\leq}7\%$ non-hacking by 1000 steps (Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}; Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}).
33	Exec-only seeds exhibit qualitatively different convergence patterns (PR range: 13--98\%), precluding meaningful pooling; we report seed~42 as representative of the converged regime (Table~\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44; plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan001-exec-only-s43-1000-r7, plan001-exec-only-s44-1000-r2
34	Two additional seeds under identical conditions fail to converge ($\text{PR}{\approx}14\%$; Appendix~\ref{app:seed-convergence}), retaining genuine reasoning and suggesting that structural degeneration requires successful RL optimization as a prerequisite (Appendix~\ref{app:llama-coldstart}).
35	
36	\textbf{SFT baseline.}
37	SFT on gold formalizations produces $92\%$ non-hacking outputs at $\text{PR}{=}13\%$ ($\text{OR}{=}147$ vs.\ exec-only, $p{<}0.001$), confirming that hacking modes are absent before RL optimization.
38	SFT serves as a pre-RL reference rather than a PR-matched control; its low PR reflects minimal optimization pressure.
39	Early-training comparison supports this interpretation: at 100 steps ($\text{PR}{=}32\%$), hacking already reaches $31\%$ vs.\ SFT's $8\%$ at comparable PR (Appendix~\ref{app:100step}). %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-1000/s43-1000/s44-1000-r3
40	
41	\subsection{Goodhart Effects on ISR-as-Reward}
42	\label{sec:goodhart}
43	
44	We reverse ISR's role from observer to reward: $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1 + \beta\,\text{ISR})$, with matched random control ($u\sim\mathcal{U}(0,1)$).
45	The result is a regressional Goodhart pattern~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: optimizing a structural proxy shifts the \emph{composition} of failure modes without reducing their aggregate prevalence.
46	ISR reward systematically selects for tautological shortcuts (TS) over conclusion embedding (CE) rather than producing genuine reasoning (Table~\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}). The scalar ISR$^*$ advantage ($214\times$ raw) collapses to $1.06\times$ after controlling for premise count; the compositional mode shift provides the primary evidence.
47	
48	\paragraph{Multiplicative coupling.}
49	The multiplicative reward formulation conflates ISR signal with interaction effects; three controls constrain this confound: exec-only's convergence to pure hacking, the qualitative TS shift under ISR, and an additive variant that did not converge (Appendix~\ref{app:setup}); the shuffled-ISR control partially addresses the coupling by preserving ISR's distribution while breaking sample-level semantics.
50	The shuffled-ISR control produces SFT-like outcomes ($87\%$ non-hacking at low PR), consistent with the Goodhart effect requiring the sample-level ISR signal, not its distributional shape (corroborating rather than definitive given $N{=}46$ proved samples).
51

66	Shuffled-ISR & 0.1 & 15{\scriptsize$\pm$1} & 0.559{\scriptsize$\pm$.02} & 7 & 7 & \textbf{87} \\ %% registry: d157-shuffled-isr-s42-r3/s43/s44 (3-seed pooled, D159)

Multi-seed rows report pooled TS/CE/N-H rates and PR/ISR$^*$ as mean$\pm$std. \textbf{Markers:} $^\dagger$Seed~42 only; seed~43 achieves $\text{PR}{=}13\%$ (nucleus sampling); seed~44 achieves $\text{PR}{=}98\%$ with $100\%$ TS. Per-seed data in Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}. $^\ddagger$ISR$^*$ raw mean inflated by TS gaming; $1.06\times$ is the premise-count-matched value relative to random (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}, Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}); 5-seed raw ISR$^*$ in parentheses (same confound, no matched control). $^\P$Includes TS classifier false-negative correction (s44) and PG premise-subset gaming (s42; 5.2\% of proved; Appendix~\ref{app:h8}). $^\S$Random TS driven entirely by seed~43 (38.3\%); seeds~42/44: 0\%. ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ 3-seed row reports the initial experiment (s42--44); 5-seed row adds s45/s46 as replication, revealing seed dependence (${\sim}55\%$ non-hacking each; Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}). ISR$^*$ reported for completeness; mode-level analysis (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}) provides the primary diagnostic.}
73	\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}

75	
76	\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\beta{=}0.1$).}
77	ISR reward amplifies TS convergence: $71\%$ of proved samples under ISR exhibit tautological shortcuts vs.\ $14\%$ under random (Appendix~\ref{app:statistical-details}). %% registry: plan002 vs plan018 all seeds; D095 FINAL
78	TS is the canonical Goodhart move: a single premise identical to the conclusion yields $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ by construction, directly satisfying the LOO metric (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}).
79	Total conclusion embedding ($\text{CE}+\text{TS}$) spans $68$--$100\%$ across both conditions; the composition shifts (CE dominates under random, TS under ISR) without reducing aggregate hacking (Figure~\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).
80	A seed-stratified $\chi^2$ test confirms the overall mode distribution differs significantly between ISR and random conditions ($\chi^2{=}174.8$, $\text{df}{=}2$, $p{<}10^{-38}$; Appendix~\ref{app:statistical-details}).
81	
82	\textbf{Seed-dependent outcomes.}
83	ISR reward produces bimodal outcomes: 3/5 seeds converge to gaming ($\text{N-H}{\leq}6\%$), while 2/5 retain substantial genuine reasoning (${\sim}55\%$), demonstrating that the Goodhart effect is seed-dependent rather than universal (seeds~45/46 added post-hoc to test replication; Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}).
84	
85	\textbf{Premise-count confound.}
86	The $214\times$ raw ISR$^*$ collapse is largely explained by premise-count reduction: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations that mechanically inflate ISR.
87	The premise-count-matched comparison yields only $1.06\times$ (McNemar $p{=}0.317$, $n{=}100$ matched pairs; Appendix~\ref{app:premise-count}), indicating that the scalar ISR advantage is not statistically significant.
88	
89	The compositional evidence, however, survives this control.
90	In the modal premise-count range (4--6 premises, where most proved samples fall), TS reaches $19.7\%$ under ISR versus $0\%$ under random reward (Table~\ref{tab:premise-bin-modes}).
91	TS hacking is mechanistically limited to simpler problems---models cannot restate the conclusion while ignoring all premises when formalization complexity is high.
92	This complexity dependence is a mechanistic insight, not a confound: the compositional mode shift---not the scalar ISR difference---constitutes the primary evidence for the Goodhart effect.
93	The failure of ISR-as-reward demonstrates that structural diagnostics cannot be naively repurposed as training objectives---a finding that applies to any LOO-based metric in solver-verified settings.
94	
95	\textbf{Higher doses and temporal dynamics.}
96	Any genuine reasoning under ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ is transient: non-hacking drops from $17\%$ to $2\%$ by 1000 steps as TS escalates (Figure~\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}). %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42
97	Higher doses accelerate collapse: $\beta{=}0.2$ eliminates all non-hacking; $\beta{=}0.3$ destabilizes training itself, producing multi-modal failure~\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization} across both ISR and random conditions (Appendix~\ref{app:beta03}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b02-s42/s43/s44, plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44
98	
99	\subsection{Cross-Domain and Cross-Dataset Evidence}
100	\label{sec:crossdomain}
101	\label{sec:crosslogic}
102	
103	\begin{figure}[t]
104	\centering
105	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}
106	\caption{Cross-logic results. \textbf{Top}: FOL vs.\ Prolog exec-only $\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$; $^\dagger$s43 FOL uses sampling $T{=}0.7$, not strictly comparable). \textbf{Bottom}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition; all 3 seeds reach 0\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}
107	\label{fig:prolog-comparison}
108	\end{figure}
109	
110	\textbf{Prolog: universal Goodhart.}
111	Replicating the pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog (Figure~\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}), exec-only seeds collapse to $\text{ISR}^*{\approx}0$ via redundant ground-fact coding, supporting STA's diagnostic transfer across logic types.
112	Under ISR-as-reward, all three seeds reach $0\%$ non-hacking through distinct mechanisms: clean TS (s42), degenerate syntax bypassing text-match classifiers (s43), and query-as-fact gaming (s44; Table~\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}).
113	Prolog's simpler semantics broadens the gaming basin, eliminating the residual genuine reasoning seen in FOL.
114	
115	\textbf{ProofFOL exec-only: cross-dataset replication.}
116	Converged seeds collapse to near-zero genuine reasoning, confirming STA's diagnostic across dataset families.
117	The mode repertoire differs across datasets: among converged seeds, TS dominates one while CE dominates the other, showing that mode repertoire is dataset-specific while the degeneration phenomenon is universal.
118	
119	\textbf{ProofFOL ISR ($\beta{=}0.1$): dataset-dependent asymmetry.}
120	ISR reward on ProofFOL produces directionally higher genuine rates than exec-only ($p{=}0.10$, exploratory, underpowered; directional only; Table~\ref{tab:prooffol-detailed}), though at lower PR, possibly reflecting insufficient training.
121	
122	\textbf{Mechanism.}
123	The asymmetry follows from hacking geometry: on FOLIO, tautological shortcuts yield high ISR (regressional Goodhart~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}); on ProofFOL, the dominant hacking mode embeds conclusions among many premises, yielding $\text{ISR}{\approx}0$ and rendering ISR reward effectively corrective.
124	ISR-as-reward inherits the dataset's hacking topology.
125	
126	\paragraph{Cross-scale validation (Qwen3-8B).}
127	Qwen3-8B does not converge under exec-only GRPO ($\text{PR}{=}14\%$, $K{=}8$; $K{=}4$ produces zero gradient), consistent with degeneration requiring successful convergence (Appendix~\ref{app:8b-results}).
128

163	Three modes account for the vast majority of hacking (Table~\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; Appendix~\ref{app:hacking-modes}): CE (conclusion embedding), TS (conclusion duplication), and PG (premise-subset gaming).
164	The central pattern: \emph{reward formulation determines which mode dominates but not whether hacking occurs}; all GRPO conditions produce overwhelming degeneration, while SFT achieves $92\%$ non-hacking.
```

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Execution-based evaluation of neurosymbolic reasoning conflates faithful derivation with structural shortcuts: a formalization that trivially embeds the conclusion among its premises proves just as reliably as a genuine one, and RL optimizers exploit this indifference.
3	We propose \emph{component necessity} as a diagnostic principle and operationalize it as \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA): for each proved output, STA tests whether every component is individually required for proof success, yielding the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).
4	ISR reveals that prove-rate gains conceal a compositional shift toward hacking modes, but ISR occupies a paradoxical position: the same leave-one-out structure that makes it informative also makes it gameable.
5	Tautological formalizations satisfy the necessity criterion by construction, so when ISR is repurposed as a training reward, the model learns to game the diagnostic rather than the task.
6	In our Qwen3-4B experiments, the resulting Goodhart effect is seed-dependent---3/5 seeds converge to gaming, 2/5 retain genuine reasoning---but the \emph{observe-vs-optimize asymmetry} persists across logic types and dataset families: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.\footnote{Code and data: \url{[anonymized]}}
7	\end{abstract}
8	
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	A neurosymbolic reasoner can achieve rising prove rate while the internal structure of its proofs collapses entirely.
5	The root cause is evaluation by a single criterion---whether the generated formalization proves under a formal solver---which treats all proofs as equal: a formalization that embeds the conclusion verbatim among its premises receives the same reward as one that derives it through genuine reasoning.
6	Under reinforcement learning, this indifference becomes an active vulnerability~\citep{geirhos2020shortcut}; the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}, and execution feedback provides no signal against structural shortcuts.
7	
8	\paragraph{The evaluation gap.}
9	Neurosymbolic formalizers~\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm} and RL-trained variants~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseekmath} rely on execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for whether individual components contribute to the proof.
10	Process reward models~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} offer finer granularity but require expensive step-level annotations that do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.
11	Inference-time gaming detection~\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming} detects gaming at inference time but offers no training-time diagnostic.
12	What is missing is an annotation-free method for testing \emph{component necessity} in neurosymbolic outputs.
13	
14	\paragraph{Component necessity testing.}
15	We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic that tests whether each component of a formalization is necessary for proof success.
16	For each proved output, STA removes components one at a time and re-runs the solver; the fraction whose removal breaks the proof yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).
17	A formalization where every component is necessary receives $\text{ISR}{=}1$; one that proves regardless of which component is removed receives $\text{ISR}{=}0$.
18	Leave-one-out ablation itself is standard~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; our contribution is its systematic application to neurosymbolic outputs, where it reveals a diagnostic--optimization asymmetry (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}--\ref{sec:goodhart}).
19	
20	\begin{figure}[t]
21	\centering
22	\includegraphics[width=0.95\columnwidth]{figures/fig5_training_isr_curves.pdf}
23	\caption{ISR$^*$ (proved samples only) training trajectories across reward conditions (rolling $w{=}20$). Exec-only collapses to $\text{ISR}{\approx}0$, confirming structural degeneration (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); the ${\sim}0.5$ plateau is an $n{=}2$ artifact. ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ seeds rise toward $\text{ISR}{\approx}1$ via TS gaming (Goodhart effect, \S\ref{sec:goodhart}); random remains low.}
24	\label{fig:training-isr-curves}
25	\end{figure}
26	
27	\paragraph{The observe-vs-optimize asymmetry.}
28	Applied post-hoc, ISR reveals that rising prove rate conceals a compositional shift in proof structure: genuine reasoning gives way to hacking modes---conclusion embedding and tautological shortcuts---invisible to execution-based metrics (Figure~\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}; \S\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
29	Yet the same LOO structure that makes ISR diagnostic also creates a Goodhart vulnerability~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}: tautological formalizations ($P{\to}P$) satisfy the necessity criterion by construction, so when ISR is repurposed as a training reward, the model learns to game the diagnostic rather than the task.
30	In our Qwen3-4B experiments, the asymmetry persists across logic types, dataset families, reward doses, and random seeds, suggesting that structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.
31	
32	\paragraph{Contributions.}
33	We contribute:
34	(\textbf{C1})~STA, a diagnostic method applicable to any solver-verified reasoning system, including the ISR metric, an eight-mode hacking taxonomy, and a cross-domain evaluation protocol (\S\S\ref{sec:sta}--\ref{sec:crossdomain});
35	(\textbf{C2})~in Qwen3-4B experiments across two logic types and multiple reward conditions, empirical characterization of a seed-dependent Goodhart effect when ISR is repurposed as a training reward: 3/5 seeds converge to gaming while 2/5 retain substantial genuine reasoning, with controls ruling out confounds (\S\ref{sec:goodhart});
36	(\textbf{C3})~in our Qwen3-4B experiments, cross-logic (FOL/Prolog) and cross-benchmark (FOLIO/ProofFOL) evidence characterizing the structural manifestation of Goodhart's law in neurosymbolic RLVR (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
37	
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation; repurposed as reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent Goodhart effect, documenting that structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize (Appendix~\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).
5	STA is applicable in principle to any RL system whose output is verified by a deterministic checker---math reasoning with formal verifiers, code generation with test suites---wherever solver-verifiable structure can be ablated.
6	Cross-logic (Prolog) and cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) experiments confirm that the compositional mode shift generalizes beyond FOL-FOLIO, though the dominant hacking mode varies by dataset (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
7	Our cross-scale results hint at a capacity-dependent pattern: structural degeneration may require model capacity sufficient for RL convergence but insufficient to resist structural shortcuts. The high non-convergence rate among exec-only seeds (two of five fail to converge; Appendix~\ref{app:seed-convergence}) further suggests degeneration requires successful convergence as a prerequisite.
8	As an aggregate metric, ISR is susceptible to premise-count confounds (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); per-sample mode classification offers a more reliable diagnostic pathway for future work.
9	Combining STA with inference-time gaming detection~\citep{kim-game-formalization, helff2026gaming} could extend structural monitoring across the training--deployment spectrum.
10	
```

```json
{
```

```json
  "parentUuid": "4bac7084-9118-4f1d-ab98-1514106dd181",

    "oldString": "persists across logic types and dataset families: structural proxies",
    "newString": "persists across logic types and dataset families---confirmed by cross-logic (Prolog) and cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) replication: structural proxies",
    "originalFile": "\\begin{abstract}\nExecution-based evaluation of neurosymbolic reasoning conflates faithful derivation with structural shortcuts: a formalization that trivially embeds the conclusion among its premises proves just as reliably as a genuine one, and RL optimizers exploit this indifference.\nWe propose \\emph{component necessity} as a diagnostic principle and operationalize it as \\emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA): for each proved output, STA tests whether every component is individually required for proof success, yielding the \\emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).\nISR reveals that prove-rate gains conceal a compositional shift toward hacking modes, but ISR occupies a paradoxical position: the same leave-one-out structure that makes it informative also makes it gameable.\nTautological formalizations satisfy the necessity criterion by construction, so when ISR is repurposed as a training reward, the model learns to game the diagnostic rather than the task.\nIn our Qwen3-4B experiments, the resulting Goodhart effect is seed-dependent---3/5 seeds converge to gaming, 2/5 retain genuine reasoning---but the \\emph{observe-vs-optimize asymmetry} persists across logic types and dataset families: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.\\footnote{Code and data: \\url{[anonymized]}}\n\\end{abstract}\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 3,
        "oldLines": 5,
        "newStart": 3,
        "newLines": 5,

          " We propose \\emph{component necessity} as a diagnostic principle and operationalize it as \\emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA): for each proved output, STA tests whether every component is individually required for proof success, yielding the \\emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).",
          " ISR reveals that prove-rate gains conceal a compositional shift toward hacking modes, but ISR occupies a paradoxical position: the same leave-one-out structure that makes it informative also makes it gameable.",
          " Tautological formalizations satisfy the necessity criterion by construction, so when ISR is repurposed as a training reward, the model learns to game the diagnostic rather than the task.",
          "-In our Qwen3-4B experiments, the resulting Goodhart effect is seed-dependent---3/5 seeds converge to gaming, 2/5 retain genuine reasoning---but the \\emph{observe-vs-optimize asymmetry} persists across logic types and dataset families: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.\\footnote{Code and data: \\url{[anonymized]}}",
          "+In our Qwen3-4B experiments, the resulting Goodhart effect is seed-dependent---3/5 seeds converge to gaming, 2/5 retain genuine reasoning---but the \\emph{observe-vs-optimize asymmetry} persists across logic types and dataset families---confirmed by cross-logic (Prolog) and cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) replication: structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.\\footnote{Code and data: \\url{[anonymized]}}",
```

```json
{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "c18e9d7e-fee9-4063-bfbc-6087bfa42673",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "2874d217-d02e-4a5d-8f12-a0b8a75888a5",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "703c4570-333c-40ea-b79c-bf51624ce6c5",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "oldString": "(\\textbf{C2})~in Qwen3-4B experiments across two logic types and multiple reward conditions, empirical characterization of a seed-dependent Goodhart effect when ISR is repurposed as a training reward: 3/5 seeds converge to gaming while 2/5 retain substantial genuine reasoning, with controls ruling out confounds (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart});",
    "newString": "(\\textbf{C2})~empirical characterization of a seed-dependent Goodhart effect when ISR is repurposed as reward, with matched controls ruling out confounds (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart});",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nA neurosymbolic reasoner can achieve rising prove rate while the internal structure of its proofs collapses entirely.\nThe root cause is evaluation by a single criterion---whether the generated formalization proves under a formal solver---which treats all proofs as equal: a formalization that embeds the conclusion verbatim among its premises receives the same reward as one that derives it through genuine reasoning.\nUnder reinforcement learning, this indifference becomes an active vulnerability~\\citep{geirhos2020shortcut}; the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}, and execution feedback provides no signal against structural shortcuts.\n\n\\paragraph{The evaluation gap.}\nNeurosymbolic formalizers~\\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm} and RL-trained variants~\\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseekmath} rely on execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for whether individual components contribute to the proof.\nProcess reward models~\\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} offer finer granularity but require expensive step-level annotations that do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.\nInference-time gaming detection~\\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming} detects gaming at inference time but offers no training-time diagnostic.\nWhat is missing is an annotation-free method for testing \\emph{component necessity} in neurosymbolic outputs.\n\n\\paragraph{Component necessity testing.}\nWe address this gap with \\emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic that tests whether each component of a formalization is necessary for proof success.\nFor each proved output, STA removes components one at a time and re-runs the solver; the fraction whose removal breaks the proof yields the \\emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).\nA formalization where every component is necessary receives $\\text{ISR}{=}1$; one that proves regardless of which component is removed receives $\\text{ISR}{=}0$.\nLeave-one-out ablation itself is standard~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; our contribution is its systematic application to neurosymbolic outputs, where it reveals a diagnostic--optimization asymmetry (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}--\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig5_training_isr_curves.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR$^*$ (proved samples only) training trajectories across reward conditions (rolling $w{=}20$). Exec-only collapses to $\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0$, confirming structural degeneration (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); the ${\\sim}0.5$ plateau is an $n{=}2$ artifact. ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seeds rise toward $\\text{ISR}{\\approx}1$ via TS gaming (Goodhart effect, \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); random remains low.}\n\\label{fig:training-isr-curves}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{The observe-vs-optimize asymmetry.}\nApplied post-hoc, ISR reveals that rising prove rate conceals a compositional shift in proof structure: genuine reasoning gives way to hacking modes---conclusion embedding and tautological shortcuts---invisible to execution-based metrics (Figure~\\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}; \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}).\nYet the same LOO structure that makes ISR diagnostic also creates a Goodhart vulnerability~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}: tautological formalizations ($P{\\to}P$) satisfy the necessity criterion by construction, so when ISR is repurposed as a training reward, the model learns to game the diagnostic rather than the task.\nIn our Qwen3-4B experiments, the asymmetry persists across logic types, dataset families, reward doses, and random seeds, suggesting that structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize.\n\n\\paragraph{Contributions.}\nWe contribute:\n(\\textbf{C1})~STA, a diagnostic method applicable to any solver-verified reasoning system, including the ISR metric, an eight-mode hacking taxonomy, and a cross-domain evaluation protocol (\\S\\S\\ref{sec:sta}--\\ref{sec:crossdomain});\n(\\textbf{C2})~in Qwen3-4B experiments across two logic types and multiple reward conditions, empirical characterization of a seed-dependent Goodhart effect when ISR is repurposed as a training reward: 3/5 seeds converge to gaming while 2/5 retain substantial genuine reasoning, with controls ruling out confounds (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart});\n(\\textbf{C3})~in our Qwen3-4B experiments, cross-logic (FOL/Prolog) and cross-benchmark (FOLIO/ProofFOL) evidence characterizing the structural manifestation of Goodhart's law in neurosymbolic RLVR (\\S\\ref{sec:crossdomain}).\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 32,
        "oldLines": 5,
        "newStart": 32,
        "newLines": 5,
        "lines": [
          " \\paragraph{Contributions.}",
          " We contribute:",
          " (\\textbf{C1})~STA, a diagnostic method applicable to any solver-verified reasoning system, including the ISR metric, an eight-mode hacking taxonomy, and a cross-domain evaluation protocol (\\S\\S\\ref{sec:sta}--\\ref{sec:crossdomain});",
          "-(\\textbf{C2})~in Qwen3-4B experiments across two logic types and multiple reward conditions, empirical characterization of a seed-dependent Goodhart effect when ISR is repurposed as a training reward: 3/5 seeds converge to gaming while 2/5 retain substantial genuine reasoning, with controls ruling out confounds (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart});",
          "+(\\textbf{C2})~empirical characterization of a seed-dependent Goodhart effect when ISR is repurposed as reward, with matched controls ruling out confounds (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart});",
          " (\\textbf{C3})~in our Qwen3-4B experiments, cross-logic (FOL/Prolog) and cross-benchmark (FOLIO/ProofFOL) evidence characterizing the structural manifestation of Goodhart's law in neurosymbolic RLVR (\\S\\ref{sec:crossdomain})."
        ]
```

```json
    "newString": "STA is applicable in principle to any RL system whose output is verified by a deterministic checker---math reasoning with formal verifiers, code generation with test suites---though empirical validation beyond logic programming remains future work.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nSTA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation; repurposed as reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent Goodhart effect, documenting that structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize (Appendix~\\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).\nSTA is applicable in principle to any RL system whose output is verified by a deterministic checker---math reasoning with formal verifiers, code generation with test suites---wherever solver-verifiable structure can be ablated.\nCross-logic (Prolog) and cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) experiments confirm that the compositional mode shift generalizes beyond FOL-FOLIO, though the dominant hacking mode varies by dataset (\\S\\ref{sec:crossdomain}).\nOur cross-scale results hint at a capacity-dependent pattern: structural degeneration may require model capacity sufficient for RL convergence but insufficient to resist structural shortcuts. The high non-convergence rate among exec-only seeds (two of five fail to converge; Appendix~\\ref{app:seed-convergence}) further suggests degeneration requires successful convergence as a prerequisite.\nAs an aggregate metric, ISR is susceptible to premise-count confounds (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); per-sample mode classification offers a more reliable diagnostic pathway for future work.\nCombining STA with inference-time gaming detection~\\citep{kim-game-formalization, helff2026gaming} could extend structural monitoring across the training--deployment spectrum.\n",
```

```text
Links: [{"title":"[2209.13085] Defining and Characterizing Reward Hacking","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.13085"},{"title":"Joar Skalse, Nikolaus H R Howe, Dmitrii Krasheninnikov, David Krueger · Defining and Characterizing Reward Hacking","url":"https://slideslive.com/38989954/defining-and-characterizing-reward-hacking"},{"title":"Defining and Characterizing Reward Hacking Joar Skalse∗ University of Oxford","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2209.13085"},{"title":"Defining and characterizing reward hacking | Proceedings of the 36th International Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems","url":"https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.5555/3600270.3600957"},{"title":"Defining and Characterizing Reward Hacking Joar Skalse∗ University of Oxford","url":"https://openreview.net/pdf?id=yb3HOXO3lX2"},{"title":"[PDF] Defining and Characterizing Reward Hacking | Semantic Scholar","url":"https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/Defining-and-Characterizing-Reward-Hacking-Skalse-Howe/004357dd9bbf3012c8fe0ccada4da401bf85dfff"},{"title":"Reward Hacking as Equilibrium under Finite Evaluation","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2603.28063"},{"title":"Defining and Characterizing Reward Hacking Joar Skalse∗ University of Oxford","url":"https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2022/file/3d719fee332caa23d5038b8a90e81796-Paper-Conference.pdf"}]

I found the research you're looking for. The paper "Defining and Characterizing Reward Hacking" by Skalse and colleagues was presented at the 36th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2022).

The authors provide the first formal definition of reward hacking, a phenomenon where optimizing an imperfect proxy reward function leads to poor performance according to the true reward function. They define a proxy as unhackable if increasing the expected proxy return can never decrease the expected true return.

One of the key findings from the research is that for the set of all stochastic policies, two reward functions can only be unhackable if one of them is constant. However, non-trivial unhackable pairs always exist when considering deterministic policies and finite sets of stochastic policies.

The paper is available on arxiv (2209.13085) and through various academic sources including OpenReview and the NeurIPS proceedings.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
I found the paper! Here's what I can tell you about it:

The paper is titled "Training Language Models to Use Prolog as a Tool" by Niklas Mellgren, Peter Schneider-Kamp, and Lukas Galke Poech, submitted on December 8, 2025.

The research investigates fine-tuning models to use Prolog as an external tool for verifiable computation. Using Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), they fine-tune Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct on a cleaned GSM8K-Prolog-Prover dataset while varying prompt structure, reward composition, and inference protocol.

Their reinforcement learning approach outperforms supervised fine-tuning, with their 3B model achieving zero-shot MMLU performance comparable to 7B few-shot results.

The paper is available on arXiv at: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07407
```

```text
I found information about this paper. Here are the key details:

**Paper Title:** "Catastrophic Goodhart: regularizing RLHF with KL divergence does not mitigate heavy-tailed reward misspecification"

**Authors:** Thomas Kwa, Drake Thomas, and Adrià Garriga-Alonso

**Venue:** Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 37 (NeurIPS 2024) Main Conference Track

**Main Findings:** When applying reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF), the reward is learned from data and therefore always has some error. It is common to mitigate this by regularizing the policy with KL divergence from a base model, with the hope that balancing reward with regularization will achieve desirable outcomes despite this reward misspecification. However, if error is heavy-tailed, some policies obtain arbitrarily high reward despite achieving no more utility than the base model--a phenomenon the authors call catastrophic Goodhart.

**Key Results:** The authors adapted a discrete optimization method to measure the tails of reward models, finding that they are consistent with light-tailed error.

The paper has been published on arXiv and presented at NeurIPS 2024.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
Based on the search results, here's information about DeepSeek-Prover-V1.5 (arXiv 2408.08152):

## Overview

DeepSeek-Prover-V1.5 is an open-source language model designed for theorem proving in Lean 4, which enhances DeepSeek-Prover-V1 by optimizing both training and inference processes.

## Training and Development

The model is pre-trained on DeepSeekMath-Base with specialization in formal mathematical languages, and undergoes supervised fine-tuning using an enhanced formal theorem proving dataset derived from DeepSeek-Prover-V1. Further refinement is achieved through reinforcement learning from proof assistant feedback (RLPAF).

## Novel Approach

Beyond the single-pass whole-proof generation approach of DeepSeek-Prover-V1, the authors propose RMaxTS, a variant of Monte-Carlo tree search that employs an intrinsic-reward-driven exploration strategy to generate diverse proof paths.

## Results

DeepSeek-Prover-V1.5 demonstrates significant improvements over DeepSeek-Prover-V1, achieving new state-of-the-art results on the test set of the high school level miniF2F benchmark (63.5%) and the undergraduate level ProofNet benchmark (25.3%).

The paper was submitted to arXiv on August 15, 2024, and the corresponding code and model are available on GitHub and Hugging Face.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
Based on the search results, here's information about DeepSeek-Prover-V2 (arXiv 2504.21801):

## Overview

DeepSeek-Prover-V2 is an open-source large language model designed for formal theorem proving in Lean 4, with initialization data collected through a recursive theorem proving pipeline powered by DeepSeek-V3.

## Key Approach

The cold-start training procedure begins by prompting DeepSeek-V3 to decompose complex problems into a series of subgoals. The proofs of resolved subgoals are synthesized into a chain-of-thought process, combined with DeepSeek-V3's step-by-step reasoning, to create an initial cold start for reinforcement learning.

## Performance

The resulting model, DeepSeek-Prover-V2-671B, achieves state-of-the-art performance in neural theorem proving, reaching 88.9% pass ratio on the MiniF2F-test and solving 49 out of 658 problems from PutnamBench.

## Method Overview

The key innovation is its ability to identify important intermediate steps (subgoals) that lead to the final proof. This process enables the integration of both informal and formal mathematical reasoning into a unified model.
```

```text
I found the Qwen3 Technical Report (arXiv 2505.09388). Here's what the paper covers:

## Overview

Qwen3 is the latest version of the Qwen model family, comprising a series of large language models (LLMs) designed to advance performance, efficiency, and multilingual capabilities.

## Model Architecture and Scale

The Qwen3 series includes models of both dense and Mixture-of-Expert (MoE) architectures, with parameter scales ranging from 0.6 to 235 billion.

## Key Innovations

A key innovation in Qwen3 is the integration of thinking mode (for complex, multi-step reasoning) and non-thinking mode (for rapid, context-driven responses) into a unified framework, which eliminates the need to switch between different models and enables dynamic mode switching based on user queries or chat templates.

Qwen3 introduces a thinking budget mechanism, allowing users to allocate computational resources adaptively during inference, thereby balancing latency and performance based on task complexity.

## Performance

The post-trained models perform competitively against leading proprietary models and large mixture-of-experts models such as o1, o3-mini, and DeepSeek-V3, with models excelling in coding, mathematics, and agent-related tasks.

The paper was submitted to arXiv on May 14, 2025, and is publicly available under Apache 2.0.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
Based on the search results, I can provide you with comprehensive information about SatLM:

## Overview

SatLM (Satisfiability-Aided Language Modeling) is a new approach for improving the reasoning capabilities of LLMs. The paper was presented at NeurIPS 2023, with authors Xi Ye, Qiaochu Chen, Isil Dillig, and Greg Durrett.

## Approach

The core idea is to cast a natural language reasoning problem as a satisfiability (SAT) problem by parsing an NL input into a declarative task specification (a set of logical constraints) using prompting, then using a SAT solver to solve the problem.

## Key Advantages

The declarative specification is closer to the problem description than the reasoning steps are, so the LLM can parse it more accurately. Furthermore, by offloading the actual reasoning task to an automated theorem prover, the approach can guarantee the correctness of the answer with respect to the parsed specification and avoid planning errors in the reasoning process.

## Performance Results

The approach was evaluated on 8 different datasets and consistently outperforms program-aided LMs in the imperative paradigm. In particular, SatLM outperforms program-aided LMs by 23% on a challenging subset of the GSM arithmetic reasoning dataset; SatLM also achieves a new state-of-the-art on LSAT and BoardgameQA, surpassing previous models that are trained on the respective training sets.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Neurosymbolic reasoning.}
5	LLM-solver pipelines~\citep{satlm, logic-lm, linc, fover-pei2025, lorp} and RL methods trained on solver feedback~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseek-prover-v15, deepseek-r1} evaluate execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for component necessity.
6	AlphaProof~\citep{alphaproof2025} and DeepSeek-Prover-V2~\citep{deepseek-prover-v2} apply RL-based formal verification at scale in Lean~4 for competition mathematics; STA-style diagnostics could probe whether similar structural degeneration occurs in these settings.
7	We use FOLIO~\citep{folio}, the only public NLI benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations.
8	
9	\paragraph{Reward hacking and Goodhart's Law.}
10	Goodhart's Law is established theoretically~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, skalse-reward-hacking, karwowski-goodhart-rl} and empirically in RLHF~\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization} and logical formalization~\citep{bondarenko-spec-gaming, kim-game-formalization}.
11	Most closely related, \citet{kim-game-formalization} document inference-time gaming on FOLIO with frontier models and Lean~4; our work is complementary in studying training-time degeneration with component necessity rather than faithfulness, and combining STA with inference-time detection would provide full gaming coverage.
12	\citet{kwa-catastrophic-goodhart} show that heavy-tailed reward misspecification causes catastrophic Goodhart where KL regularization provably fails, a theoretical lens for why GRPO's KL penalty does not prevent our observed collapse.
13	Reward model ensembles~\citep{coste2023reward} and constrained optimization~\citep{moskovitz2023confronting} address overoptimization at the reward level; STA instead diagnoses structural consequences at the output level.
14	Concurrent work documents spurious signals in RLVR~\citep{spurious-rewards, yan2026spurious, dong2026probing, metr-reward-hacking} and proposes composite rewards as mitigations~\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond}; \citet{yue2025rl-reasoning} show RLVR redistributes base-model probability mass rather than creating new reasoning, consistent with our finding that GRPO exploits existing shortcuts.
15	\citet{helff2026gaming} propose Input Perturbation Testing (IPT) for gaming detection; STA shares the perturbation intuition but operates at the component level in neurosymbolic settings where gaming emerges during training.
16	While IPT perturbs \emph{inputs} to detect inference-time gaming, STA performs leave-one-out ablation on \emph{solver-input components} to diagnose training-time structural degeneration, operating at component-level rather than input-level granularity and targeting the training process rather than deployment behavior.
17	\citet{khalifa2026countdown} provide controlled testbeds showing that reward hacking in Countdown emerges progressively during training; STA complements such testbeds by offering a component-level diagnostic applicable to neurosymbolic pipelines.
18	\citet{nishimura2026specgaming} systematically benchmark specification gaming across reasoning models, finding that RL training substantially increases exploit rates; our STA results provide a mechanistic view of how such gaming manifests as structural degeneration in solver traces.
19	Our Goodhart effect follows the regressional pattern~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} on FOLIO, with extremal dynamics at high $\beta$; its application to a diagnostic designed to \emph{detect} gaming, and the dataset-dependent asymmetry it reveals, are to our knowledge novel.
20	Complementarily, \citet{wilhelm2026monitoring} detect reward hacking at generation time via internal activations, whereas STA provides a post-training structural diagnostic.
21	
22	\paragraph{Mutation testing.}
23	STA adapts the LOO mutation paradigm~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} to solver inputs; novelty lies in application and findings, not in the perturbation primitive.
24	Related concerns about CoT faithfulness~\citep{lanham2023faithfulness, turpin2023unfaithful} operate at the reasoning-chain level rather than solver-input components.
25	
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation; repurposed as reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent Goodhart effect, documenting that structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize (Appendix~\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).
5	STA is applicable in principle to any RL system whose output is verified by a deterministic checker---math reasoning with formal verifiers, code generation with test suites---though empirical validation beyond logic programming remains future work.
6	Cross-logic (Prolog) and cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) experiments confirm that the compositional mode shift generalizes beyond FOL-FOLIO, though the dominant hacking mode varies by dataset (\S\ref{sec:crossdomain}).
7	Our cross-scale results hint at a capacity-dependent pattern: structural degeneration may require model capacity sufficient for RL convergence but insufficient to resist structural shortcuts. The high non-convergence rate among exec-only seeds (two of five fail to converge; Appendix~\ref{app:seed-convergence}) further suggests degeneration requires successful convergence as a prerequisite.
8	As an aggregate metric, ISR is susceptible to premise-count confounds (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); per-sample mode classification offers a more reliable diagnostic pathway for future work.
9	For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc rather than as training rewards; the mode breakdown, not the scalar, provides the reliable signal.
10	Combining STA with inference-time gaming detection~\citep{kim-game-formalization, helff2026gaming} could extend structural monitoring across the training--deployment spectrum.
11	
```

```json
  "type": "user",
```

```json
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/docs/paper/conclusion.tex",
    "oldString": "For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc rather than as training rewards; the mode breakdown, not the scalar, provides the reliable signal.\nCombining STA with inference-time gaming detection~\\citep{kim-game-formalization, helff2026gaming} could extend structural monitoring across the training--deployment spectrum.",
    "newString": "For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc rather than as training rewards; the mode breakdown, not the scalar, provides the reliable signal.\nOur findings suggest several mitigation directions: mode-aware reward shaping that penalizes detected hacking patterns (CE, TS) rather than optimizing a single structural scalar; compositional rewards combining execution, structural, and anti-tautology signals~\\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond}; and curriculum strategies that increase structural pressure only after stable convergence, exploiting the capacity-dependent threshold observed in \\S\\ref{sec:crossdomain}.\nCombining STA with inference-time gaming detection~\\citep{kim-game-formalization, helff2026gaming} could extend structural monitoring across the training--deployment spectrum.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nSTA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation; repurposed as reward, ISR produces a seed-dependent Goodhart effect, documenting that structural proxies for formalization integrity are easier to observe than to optimize (Appendix~\\ref{app:practical-guidelines}).\nSTA is applicable in principle to any RL system whose output is verified by a deterministic checker---math reasoning with formal verifiers, code generation with test suites---though empirical validation beyond logic programming remains future work.\nCross-logic (Prolog) and cross-benchmark (ProofFOL) experiments confirm that the compositional mode shift generalizes beyond FOL-FOLIO, though the dominant hacking mode varies by dataset (\\S\\ref{sec:crossdomain}).\nOur cross-scale results hint at a capacity-dependent pattern: structural degeneration may require model capacity sufficient for RL convergence but insufficient to resist structural shortcuts. The high non-convergence rate among exec-only seeds (two of five fail to converge; Appendix~\\ref{app:seed-convergence}) further suggests degeneration requires successful convergence as a prerequisite.\nAs an aggregate metric, ISR is susceptible to premise-count confounds (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); per-sample mode classification offers a more reliable diagnostic pathway for future work.\nFor practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc rather than as training rewards; the mode breakdown, not the scalar, provides the reliable signal.\nCombining STA with inference-time gaming detection~\\citep{kim-game-formalization, helff2026gaming} could extend structural monitoring across the training--deployment spectrum.\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 7,
        "oldLines": 4,
        "newStart": 7,
        "newLines": 5,
        "lines": [
          " Our cross-scale results hint at a capacity-dependent pattern: structural degeneration may require model capacity sufficient for RL convergence but insufficient to resist structural shortcuts. The high non-convergence rate among exec-only seeds (two of five fail to converge; Appendix~\\ref{app:seed-convergence}) further suggests degeneration requires successful convergence as a prerequisite.",
          " As an aggregate metric, ISR is susceptible to premise-count confounds (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); per-sample mode classification offers a more reliable diagnostic pathway for future work.",
          " For practitioners, structural diagnostics should be applied post-hoc rather than as training rewards; the mode breakdown, not the scalar, provides the reliable signal.",
          "+Our findings suggest several mitigation directions: mode-aware reward shaping that penalizes detected hacking patterns (CE, TS) rather than optimizing a single structural scalar; compositional rewards combining execution, structural, and anti-tautology signals~\\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond}; and curriculum strategies that increase structural pressure only after stable convergence, exploiting the capacity-dependent threshold observed in \\S\\ref{sec:crossdomain}.",
          " Combining STA with inference-time gaming detection~\\citep{kim-game-formalization, helff2026gaming} could extend structural monitoring across the training--deployment spectrum."
        ]
```

```json
    "oldString": "\\citet{helff2026gaming} propose Input Perturbation Testing (IPT) for gaming detection; STA shares the perturbation intuition but operates at the component level in neurosymbolic settings where gaming emerges during training.\nWhile IPT perturbs \\emph{inputs} to detect inference-time gaming, STA performs leave-one-out ablation on \\emph{solver-input components} to diagnose training-time structural degeneration, operating at component-level rather than input-level granularity and targeting the training process rather than deployment behavior.",
    "newString": "\\citet{helff2026gaming} propose Input Perturbation Testing (IPT), which perturbs inputs to detect inference-time gaming; STA instead ablates solver-input components to diagnose training-time structural degeneration, operating at finer granularity and targeting the training process.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Neurosymbolic reasoning.}\nLLM-solver pipelines~\\citep{satlm, logic-lm, linc, fover-pei2025, lorp} and RL methods trained on solver feedback~\\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseek-prover-v15, deepseek-r1} evaluate execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for component necessity.\nAlphaProof~\\citep{alphaproof2025} and DeepSeek-Prover-V2~\\citep{deepseek-prover-v2} apply RL-based formal verification at scale in Lean~4 for competition mathematics; STA-style diagnostics could probe whether similar structural degeneration occurs in these settings.\nWe use FOLIO~\\citep{folio}, the only public NLI benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations.\n\n\\paragraph{Reward hacking and Goodhart's Law.}\nGoodhart's Law is established theoretically~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, skalse-reward-hacking, karwowski-goodhart-rl} and empirically in RLHF~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization} and logical formalization~\\citep{bondarenko-spec-gaming, kim-game-formalization}.\nMost closely related, \\citet{kim-game-formalization} document inference-time gaming on FOLIO with frontier models and Lean~4; our work is complementary in studying training-time degeneration with component necessity rather than faithfulness, and combining STA with inference-time detection would provide full gaming coverage.\n\\citet{kwa-catastrophic-goodhart} show that heavy-tailed reward misspecification causes catastrophic Goodhart where KL regularization provably fails, a theoretical lens for why GRPO's KL penalty does not prevent our observed collapse.\nReward model ensembles~\\citep{coste2023reward} and constrained optimization~\\citep{moskovitz2023confronting} address overoptimization at the reward level; STA instead diagnoses structural consequences at the output level.\nConcurrent work documents spurious signals in RLVR~\\citep{spurious-rewards, yan2026spurious, dong2026probing, metr-reward-hacking} and proposes composite rewards as mitigations~\\citep{tarek2025reward, pronesti2026beyond}; \\citet{yue2025rl-reasoning} show RLVR redistributes base-model probability mass rather than creating new reasoning, consistent with our finding that GRPO exploits existing shortcuts.\n\\citet{helff2026gaming} propose Input Perturbation Testing (IPT) for gaming detection; STA shares the perturbation intuition but operates at the component level in neurosymbolic settings where gaming emerges during training.\nWhile IPT perturbs \\emph{inputs} to detect inference-time gaming, STA performs leave-one-out ablation on \\emph{solver-input components} to diagnose training-time structural degeneration, operating at component-level rather than input-level granularity and targeting the training process rather than deployment behavior.\n\\citet{khalifa2026countdown} provide controlled testbeds showing that reward hacking in Countdown emerges progressively during training; STA complements such testbeds by offering a component-level diagnostic applicable to neurosymbolic pipelines.\n\\citet{nishimura2026specgaming} systematically benchmark specification gaming across reasoning models, finding that RL training substantially increases exploit rates; our STA results provide a mechanistic view of how such gaming manifests as structural degeneration in solver traces.\nOur Goodhart effect follows the regressional pattern~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} on FOLIO, with extremal dynamics at high $\\beta$; its application to a diagnostic designed to \\emph{detect} gaming, and the dataset-dependent asymmetry it reveals, are to our knowledge novel.\nComplementarily, \\citet{wilhelm2026monitoring} detect reward hacking at generation time via internal activations, whereas STA provides a post-training structural diagnostic.\n\n\\paragraph{Mutation testing.}\nSTA adapts the LOO mutation paradigm~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} to solver inputs; novelty lies in application and findings, not in the perturbation primitive.\nRelated concerns about CoT faithfulness~\\citep{lanham2023faithfulness, turpin2023unfaithful} operate at the reasoning-chain level rather than solver-input components.\n",

          "-\\citet{helff2026gaming} propose Input Perturbation Testing (IPT) for gaming detection; STA shares the perturbation intuition but operates at the component level in neurosymbolic settings where gaming emerges during training.",
```
